PKO Biegowe 360 stopni za nami. Cztery dni wiedzy, sportowych emocji i spotkań z mistrzami

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Cztery dni pełne wiedzy, inspiracji, sportowych emocji i spotkań z ludźmi, których łączy miłość do biegania – tak można podsumować konferencję PKO Biegowe 360 stopni, która odbyła się w Centralnym Ośrodku Sportu w Zakopanem. W niedzielę zakończyła się kolejna edycja wydarzenia, które zgromadziło biegaczy amatorów, trenerów, ekspertów oraz czołowych polskich lekkoatletów. Uczestnicy mogli nie tylko wysłuchać znakomitych wykładów, ale również sprawdzić zdobytą wiedzę w praktyce, wystartować w biegu z gwiazdami i porozmawiać ze swoimi sportowymi idolami.

Konferencja rozpoczęła się już w czwartek od części samorządowej, poświęconej m.in. zagadnieniom związanym z rozwojem sportu i aktywności fizycznej. Od piątku wydarzenie nabrało typowo biegowego charakteru. Przez kolejne dni uczestnicy mogli korzystać z bogatego programu wykładów, debat i spotkań z ekspertami, którzy dzielili się wiedzą na temat treningu, przygotowania motorycznego, żywienia, regeneracji, fizjoterapii oraz profilaktyki kontuzji.

Nie zabrakło również okazji do bezpośrednich spotkań z gwiazdami lekkoatletyki. W ramach porannego LOTTO Biegu Śniadaniowego uczestnicy trenowali wspólnie m.in. z Natalią Bukowiecką, Igą Baumgart-Witan, Justyną Święty-Ersetic, Sofią Ennaoui, Adamem Kszczotem, Kajetanem Duszyńskim i Piotrem Liskiem.

Zwieńczeniem soboty był natomiast PKO Dycha Pod Krokwią z Gwiazdami na dystansach 5 i 10 kilometrów. Na trasie pojawili się również sportowcy uczestniczący w konferencji, a dla Natalii Bukowieckiej był to pierwszy start na dystansie 10 kilometrów.

Niedziela, czyli ostatni dzień PKO Biegowego 360 stopni, tradycyjnie poświęcona była praktyce. Uczestnicy brali udział w zajęciach prowadzonych przez ambasadorów, trenerów i ekspertów projektu. Wśród prowadzących znaleźli się znakomici sportowcy, byli zawodnicy i specjaliści. Przygotowano dziewięć laboratoriów treningowych, które pozwalały przełożyć wiedzę zdobytą podczas wcześniejszych wykładów na konkretne ćwiczenia. Uczestnicy pracowali nad mobilnością i stabilizacją, poznawali zasady prawidłowego treningu siłowego biegacza, a także doskonalili kadencję, długość kroku i rytm biegu. Dużo uwagi poświęcono również prawidłowej rozgrzewce i rozciąganiu, technice wykonywania skipów i ćwiczeń na płotkach oraz koordynacji ruchowej.

Nie zabrakło zajęć dotyczących regeneracji i rozluźniania mięśni, a także praktycznych wskazówek, jak poprawnie pokonywać podbiegi i zbiegi. Dzięki bezpośredniej pracy z trenerami uczestnicy mogli skorygować błędy, sprawdzić swoją technikę i przekonać się, jak poszczególne elementy treningu wpływają na jakość biegania.

To bardzo ciekawe zestawienie praktyki z teorią. Dzisiaj mamy same panele praktyczne, dzięki czemu uczestnicy mogą przekuć w praktykę to, czego dowiedzieli się podczas wykładów i dyskusji. Nawet najprostsze ćwiczenia potwierdzają to, o czym mówili prelegenci – o technice biegu, sile i jej znaczeniu. Kiedy uczestnicy zaczynają wykonywać ćwiczenia i pokazujemy im, jak wpływają one na prawidłową technikę oraz poszczególne parametry biegu, rozumieją, że rzeczywiście mają one znaczenie – podkreśla Dariusz Korzeniowski, znany biegacz, jeden z trenerów podczas konferencji.

Wysoki poziom uczestników i ich zaangażowanie pozytywnie zaskoczyły również Tomasza Lewandowskiego. Trener zwrócił uwagę, że na zajęciach spotkał osoby aktywne, świadome swoich potrzeb i gotowe do dalszego rozwoju.

Jestem naprawdę pod wrażeniem. Wszyscy są otwarci, chłonni wiedzy, przychodzą z nastawieniem, żeby jak najwięcej wyciągnąć z zajęć. Zaskoczył mnie też poziom uczestników. Nie jest tak, jak myślałem, że przyjdą ludzie, których trzeba będzie dopiero uczyć podstaw. To aktywne osoby, z których każda ma już jakieś doświadczenie. Podejrzewam, że część z nich startuje już w kategoriach masters, jeździ na mistrzostwa świata i Europy. To naprawdę zapaleńcy. W tym roku na moich zajęciach było niewiele młodych osób. Rok temu pojawiali się zawodnicy w wieku 17, 18 czy 19 lat, którzy szukali wskazówek treningowych. W internecie można znaleźć teorię na temat każdego ćwiczenia, a nawet film pokazujący, jak je wykonać. Jednak to nie jest wiedza skierowana indywidualnie do nas – dodaje Tomasz Lewandowski, uznany w świecie lekkoatletyki trener biegów średnich i długich.

O tym, jak ważne jest połączenie teorii z praktyką, mówił również Adam Kszczot. Jego zdaniem właśnie podczas zajęć praktycznych uczestnicy mogą najlepiej zrozumieć, jak wykorzystać informacje zdobyte w trakcie wykładów i wprowadzić je do własnego treningu.

Trudne słowo „laboratoria”, a tak naprawdę chodzi o zajęcia praktyczne. Kiedy siedzimy na sali i chłoniemy wiedzę, czasami może być jej przytłaczająco dużo. Nawet jeśli co chwilę się śmiejemy, bo anegdoty bywają bardzo porywające, to właśnie podczas zajęć praktycznych możemy przypomnieć sobie: „Na tym wykładzie o tym mówiliśmy, a jak to działa w praktyce? Zobaczmy, jak funkcjonuje to na waszych ciałach”. Wystarczy szybka korekta, poprawa i okazuje się, że bieganie zaczyna mieć sens. Wszystkie teoretyczne elementy łączą się w jedną całość, a w każdym ćwiczeniu odnajdujemy to, o czym mówili prelegenci. Czy może być coś lepszego niż jednoczesne poznawanie teorii i praktyki? Szerokie uśmiechy, podziękowania i wspólne zdjęcia pokazują, że uczestnicy się ze sobą zżyli. To wspólnota, community – po polsku po prostu społeczność. Taką biegową społeczność Biegowego 360 stopni będę wspominał przez cały kolejny rok, aż do następnej edycji – podsumowuje Adam Kszczot, były lekkoatleta, medalista mistrzostw świata i Europy, a także współorganizator konferencji PKO Biegowe 360 stopni.

Strictly Come Dancing announces surprise format change as fourth couple exits

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

The departing celebrity was earlier criticised as ‘flat-footed and out of time’

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Shaun Wright-Phillips has become the fourth celebrity to exit this year's Strictly Come Dancing, leaving the competition with his professional partner Alexis Warr.

The former England footballer and Warr were defeated in the dance-off by actor Jaime Winstone and Carlos Gu.

Winstone and Gu had finished in the bottom two after the public vote, despite scoring 25 for their dance on Saturday's episode of the BBC show.

Sunday's results show also brought a surprise announcement: Strictly's first triple dance-off, which will take place during next week's Halloween episode.

The three couples with the lowest combined judges' scores and public votes will dance again next week, before the judges eliminate one pair.

Strictly will return to a two-couple dance-off for the rest of the series.

In the dance-off, Wright-Phillips and Warr reprised their jive to “Faith” by Stevie Wonder featuring Ariana Grande, while Winstone and Gu performed their foxtrot to “Man I Need” by Olivia Dean again.

Judges Craig Revel Horwood and Motsi Mabuse voted to save Gu and Winstone.

Head judge Shirley Ballas voted for Wright-Phillips and Warr, leaving Du Beke, who had this week's casting vote, to decide who remained on the show.

The pair had ended up in the dance-off despite finishing above former Love Island star Cach Mercer and his professional partner Maddie Ingoldsby, who scored 24.

ADVERTISEMENT. If you sign up to this service we will earn commission. This revenue helps to fund journalism across The Independent.

They also finished above Emmerdale star Lawrence Robb and Jowita Przystal, who scored just 19 on Saturday.

Robb and Przystal's performance was singled out for criticism from Revel Horwood on Saturday, who said that the soap star was “flat-footed and out of time”.

Elsewhere in this week's live show, Australian singer Delta Goodrem returned after her rib injury to score the first 10 of the series.

Her rumba to Celine Dion's “It's All Coming Back To Me Now” with professional partner Kai Widdrington scored 34.

Winter Olympic champion Tabby Stoecker topped the leaderboard on Saturday, following Goodrem by scoring two 10s for her couple's choice dance to “Stupid Song” by Olivia Rodrigo.

Her total of 37 was the highest of the series so far.

Her performance with professional partner Mark Karmalita was dedicated to her late father, and she appeared emotional after the dance.

Judge Shirley Ballas praised her as a “beautiful dancer” and called the performance “absolutely exquisite”.

The results show also featured Spice Girls star Melanie C joining the Strictly professional dancers for a fitness-inspired group dance.

The remaining couples will return to the dancefloor next week at 6.30pm on BBC One on Saturday October 31 for Strictly's Halloween special.

All episodes of the 2026 series so far are available to stream on BBC iPlayer.

Brazil votes in tense Lula vs Bolsonaro runoff with U.S. influence over Latin America at stake

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Published - October 25, 2026 07:07 pm IST - Rio De Janeiro

Brazil's President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva, the Workers' Party (PT) candidate for re-election, reacts after voting during the presidential runoff election, in Sao Bernardo do Campo, Brazil, on October 25, 2026. | Photo Credit: Reuters

Brazilians began voting in Sunday's (October 25, 2026) polarising presidential election between incumbent President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva and Flavio Bolsonaro, a contest that will determine whether the U.S. will significantly expand its growing influence in Latin America.

Polls before Sunday's (October 25) vote showed the two candidates, who need more than 50% of the vote to become Brazil's next leader in January, in a neck-and-neck race. The election pits the left-of-centre veteran politician Mr. Lula against Mr. Bolsonaro, an ally of U.S. President Donald Trump and the son of imprisoned former President Jair Bolsonaro.

Flávio Bolsonaro bested Mr. Lula by more than 2.2 million votes in the first round of the election earlier this month. More than 33 million voters out of nearly 160 million did not turn out.

The difference between the two candidates is stark. Mr. Bolsonaro and Mr. Lula have opposing ideological views on issues ranging from U.S.-Brazil relations and climate change to social welfare.

A win for Mr. Bolsonaro would signify a pivot to the right by Latin America's most populous country and its largest economy in a year when Trump allies have notched victories in Chile, Peru and Colombia.

A victory for Mr. Lula would leave Brazil and Mexico, which is the region's second-most populous country and led by President Claudia Sheinbaum, with left-of-centre leaders.

In many ways, Brazil's runoff vote resembles a rematch between the country's two polarized political forces that trigger passion as much as loathing. Four years ago, Mr. Lula beat Mr. Bolsonaro's father by the narrowest margin since the country's return to democracy in 1985. Polls suggest that this election will be even closer.

Mr. Lula, who first governed the country between 2003 and 2010, has faced a tough reelection battle because of concerns about the cost of living and security. Flávio Bolsonaro made a tough-on-crime approach the cornerstone of his campaign and pledged to bring down food costs.

Both have been hit by corruption suspicions involving family members and allies, with Mr. Bolsonaro implicated in a sprawling scandal rocking Brazil. Mr. Lula spent more than a year in prison on corruption convictions that were eventually annulled.

Mr. Bolsonaro's admission that he accepted about $12 million from disgraced former banker Daniel Vorcaro to produce a film on his father's life has cast a shadow over his campaign. He is under investigation for corruption, money laundering and tax evasion in connection with the case.

In the lead-up to the vote, Mr. Lula frequently portrayed Mr. Bolsonaro, whose father is under house arrest for attempting a coup, as a threat to democracy. Mr. Bolsonaro has promised to seek an amnesty for his father if elected.

The next President will name at least three Supreme Court justices out of 11 in his next term due to upcoming retirements. Mr. Bolsonaro has long taken aim at the country's top court — which has been rocked by a corruption scandal — and promised to push for judiciary reform.

Mr. Lula, trying to lure undecided voters, has invested in costly incentives since he lost the first round of voting. His government's most recent moves have been endorsing a debt relief program for small businesses and promising free public transportation on weekends. A win for the incumbent came with the Senate's approval of the six-day working week — a popular proposal backed by the government.

Mr. Lula has criticised the U.S. President before Brazil's election, speaking about the risks of U.S. intervention in Brazilian elections after the Trump administration imposed high tariffs against Brazilian exports, sanctioned a Supreme Court justice who chaired the investigation that ended up resulting in the sentencing of Jair Bolsonaro and classified drug-trafficking groups as foreign terrorist organizations, which experts say may have been intended to make Lula look soft on crime.

Flávio Bolsonaro has touted his proximity to the White House and insisted that Brazil needs to strike better deals with the Trump administration, including the rare minerals economy. Brazil estimates that its deposits hold up to 21 million tons, second only to China. He denies he's being helped by the U.S. government.

Voting in Brazil is conducted electronically and the result is usually available within hours after polling stations close at 5 p.m. Brasilia time (2000 GMT).

Brendon McCullum convinced England can learn from agonising defeat to Pakistan

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Brendon McCullum has vowed England will learn from yet another agonising loss which has left their bid to make next weekend’s tri-series final hanging in the balance.

Three days after Sri Lanka chased a staggering 430 in Rawalpindi, England looked set to break their duck in the tournament when they reduced Pakistan to 154 for nine in their pursuit of 242 in Lahore.

Shadab Khan had other ideas, though, as his maiden international hundred with just number 11 batter Haris Rauf for support sealed an unlikely one-wicket victory and a third defeat in a row for England.

Harry Brook’s side are now all but eliminated from the tri-series and their exit will be confirmed on Tuesday if Pakistan get beaten by Sri Lanka, who then face England on Thursday in the final group game.

McCullum admitted the past few days have been bruising for his team but, with the 50-over World Cup in southern Africa in 12 months’ time in mind, he believes this has been a worthwhile experience.

“We will learn from this,” England’s white-ball head coach said. “The boys are gutted, to be honest. They were gutted after the last game and they’re even more gutted after this one.

“These boys are immensely talented, they’re incredibly driven to perform for England, we know that we’re not the best side that we can be just yet but that’s the challenge that’s in front of us.

“There’s some good lessons and learning opportunities and we will learn from it because we are desperate to get to South Africa and have a realistic chance of challenging for that World Cup.”

Shadab’s 113 not out is the highest score by a number eight in ODIs and he impeccably marshalled the chase as Rauf faced just 19 balls in an unbroken last-wicket stand of 89 off 80 deliveries.

It meant that Ben Duckett’s 105 in England’s 241 all out – a total that looked below-par only for a collective effort from their bowlers to take them to the brink of victory – was in a losing cause.

Asked what they could have differently, McCullum replied: “It’s so fresh. We’ll look at it in the next little while, go through all the footage, the data and analytics and look at some of the options if we’re placed in that situation another time.

“They are the conversations we are having and we need to keep having. When you’ve got an inexperienced line-up and you’ve got a big World Cup in 12 months’ time and you’ve only got 20 games between now and then, you need to have that level of communication and those examples of real time experiences.”

Shadab was given out lbw to Gus Atkinson on 97 and the teams were even shaking hands, only for a hopeful review to show the all-rounder had grazed the ball with his bat.

There was more drama when the umpire failed to signal a wide from Sam Curran’s final ball of an over, prompting Rauf and Will Jacks into a confrontation as tensions rose.

Joe Root had to separate former Yorkshire team-mate Rauf from Jacks and it was the tail-end batter who had the last laugh as Pakistan, clad in pink to raise awareness for breast cancer, sealed victory with a four to leave England feeling blue.

Assessing the coming together between Rauf and Jacks, McCullum said: “It’s just cricket. Teams are playing for their country and it means a lot.

“There are pressure situations and moments where we get a little bit confrontational but it’s just the way it is. There’s no malice in it. I know Haris very well, he’s a good competitor and so is Will Jacks, and you’d expect nothing else from that.”

‘Clayface’ Ties ‘Obsession’ For A Rotten Tomatoes Audience Score Record

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

'Clayface' has a Rotten Tomatoes audience score that matches Obsession's a wild achievement for the comic book film.

→ Baca artikel asli

'You Can See Everything' surges with $19.5M at box office; 'Clayface' takes No. 1

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

DC Studios scored a modest, R-rated win with “Clayface,” landing a $24 million opening weekend, according to studio estimates Sunday. But the biggest success story in theaters remained A24’s Elizabeth Holmes documentary “You Can See Everything,” which pulled in $19.5 million in its first weekend of wide release.

The Nathan Fielder, Lance Oppenheim-directed “You Can See Everything” increased from 855 locations to 2,655. In expansion, it continued to blow away expectations. With $45.1 million in two weeks, it already ranks among the highest grossing documentaries ever.

“You Can See Everything” has turned into a sensation partly by drawing waves of Gen Z moviegoers. Its awards season prospects are also growing. If “You Can See Everything” is nominated for best picture at the Oscars, it would be the first documentary nominated for Hollywood's top prize.

“A24 invested a reported $7 million in an interesting but complicated idea without a beginning, middle or end, that’s turned into a zeitgeist film about money, dreams, truth and reality,” said David A. Gross, who publishes the box office newsletter FranchiseRe.

‘Clayface’ succeeds on a smaller scale for DC

In 3,458 theaters, Warner Bros.’ “Clayface” narrowly edged the A24 release for first place. That’s a solid start for the Gotham City villain tale and the third theatrical release for DC under the stewardship of James Gunn and Peter Safran. A bid to meld body horror with a comic book movie, it stars Tom Rhys Harries as Matt Hagen, a rising movie star who undergoes an experimental facial surgery after a mob boss disfigures him.

While such an opening would have been a flop for most superhero films, “Clayface” was budgeted at only $44 million — a fraction of the expense of most comic book films. It added $19 million internationally.

That was enough to offer some stability, and assured profitability, to DC Studios following the disappointment of “Supergirl.” Earlier this year, that film grossed just $126 million worldwide on a $170 million production budget.

Audiences gave “Clayface” an “A-” CinemaScore, a high grade for a horror release. Reviews were also largely good. The competition for horror audiences can be fierce in October, but “Clayface” may end up the top genre draw of the month.

“Clayface is a great example of studios taking chances, with DC venturing into R-rated horror and body horror, something that’s certainly not your typical comic book movie,” said Paul Dergarabedian, head of marketplace trends for Rentrak.

Last weekend’s top film, the video game adaptation “Street Fighter,” sank like a stone, dropping 72% with $8.6 million in its second weekend. The Paramount Pictures release, made for about $80 million, has grossed $46.9 million domestically in two weeks.

‘Wildwood’ and ‘Wicker’ make strong starts

“Wildwood,” the latest stop-motion animated film from the Portland, Oregon-based studio Laika, debuted in third place with $11.8 million in 2,779 locations. The film, directed by Laika chief executive Travis Knight, rode excellent reviews and strong audience scores (an “A” CinemaScore) to a notable improvement over the studio’s previous release: 2019’s “The Missing Link” (a $5.9 million opening).

But “Wildwood” is still unlikely to generate a profit, given that it cost $100 million to make. The PG-13 fantasy follows a teenage girl who enters an enchanted forest to find her abducted baby brother.

“Wicker,” a comic fable starring Olivia Colman and Alexander Skarsgård, launched with an excellent $5.8 million in 1,471 theaters. Acquired for $2.5 million out of Sundance by Black Bear Pictures, the well-reviewed “Wicker” is a quirky love story about a fisherwoman (Colman) who has the local basket maker weave her a husband (Skarsgård).

The weekend’s other new releases were either hard to find or little seen.

Taika Waititi’s critically panned “Klara and the Sun,” adapted from Kazuo Ishiguro’s novel, made just $725,000 at 880 theaters for Sony Pictures.

Amazon MGM opted not to even report grosses for Sacha Baron Cohen's poorly received mockumentary “Ali G: Who Iz I?”

Though Cohen took the rare step of conducting numerous interviews as himself, and not in character, “Ali G: Who Iz I?” was almost absent on its opening weekend. The film's theatrical footprint was shrunk considerably as Cohen faced backlash in the U.K. over cultural appropriation and racial caricatures.

With final domestic figures being released Monday, this list factors in the estimated ticket sales for Friday through Sunday at U.S. and Canadian theaters, according to Rentrak:

2. “You Can See Everything,” $19.5 million.

7. “Sense and Sensibility,” $3.4 million.

‘He’s not going to punk me. I’ll trick him’: Ali G: Who Iz I? stooge says he was in on joke but didn’t tell Sacha Baron Cohen

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Exclusive: Joshua Loudermilk says suspicions first raised during filming by gun shaped like wearable plastic bottom: ‘You’re gonna put a butt on top of a butt? You can’t run in that’

This article contains spoilers for Ali G: Who Iz I?

Joshua Loudermilk, one of two men apparently discredited by the new Ali G movie Ali G: Who Iz I?, has said that he was in on the joke, but that the film-makers didn’t realise this.

Loudermilk, a 54-year-old aspiring actor from North Carolina, has told the Guardian he recognised star Sacha Baron Cohen early in the shoot but continued pretending to be a hard-right Maga sympathiser prepared to defend the US border at all costs.

The movie involves Loudermilk being recruited by another Cohen character, the ex-Mossad terrorist expert Col Erran Morad, to help form a citizens’ warrior force to protect US borders, making use of his top-secret new weapon: a gun disguised as buttocks.

Along with fellow recruit Michael Aboud, the chair of the El Paso county Republican party, Loudermilk joins Morad on a mission to stop the birth on US soil of the baby of a fictional South American drugs kinpin. This involves abducting a woman apparently in labour, abandoning her in a deserted medical facility, kidnapping her newborn child (a lifelike prosthetic) and driving it in a zipped bag to the border in order to deliver it by drone to Mexico.

Aboud is shown taking the decision to drop the child from a great height into Mexico rather than have it touch US soil. He has since called Cohen a “liar” and a “pervert” and expressed his intention to sue. The film repeatedly stresses that both men were “not in on it”.

Loudermilk has told the Guardian he was surprised the production company did not notice social media updates he posted shortly after the shoot in which he named Cohen and expressed his excitement about the project.

But rather than being upset about his involvement, Loudermilk – who recently appeared as Squire Dap in the North Carolina Town of Hudson Fall Dinner Theatre’s production of Camelot – says he welcomes the publicity boost.

“I want to use this to my benefit,” he says. “I would hope Sacha would read this and laugh, like: ‘Hey, you got me!’ I had so much fun creating that character. I was trying to see if I was any good at acting in comedy. And I realised: ‘Oh, I think I’m good at this.’ And that’s what I wanna do for the rest of my life.”

His experience began in September 2025 when Loudermilk spotted a post on Craigslist by a body called the American Citizen Initiative (whose website is no longer active) recruiting “patriotic legal Americans” “concerned about the border crisis” who “believe that illegal immigration is out of control”.

The ad, one of dozens posted across US states simultaneously, invited “real American citizens” to “attend an exclusive, hands-on event experience where you’ll learn the truth about what’s happening on the border and how you can make a difference”. Attendees were promised $300 as well as travel expenses.

“I was like: this seems kind of weird,” says Loudermilk, who describes himself as broadly apolitical. “I don’t really care much about our borders. My mom’s from Ireland.”

Recently made redundant, his motivation for responding was primarily financial. “I know rightwing and leftwing organisations have money and will pay people to just hold a sign up. I remember thinking: I wanna get on that gravy train. I want some George Soros money. I’ll pretend to be whatever. Just let me feed my family. It could have been save the whales and I would have done it.”

In his application, Loudermilk fabricated hard-right views, mentioned that he had been a preacher and neglected to declare his ambitions to become a comedy actor – in part, he says, out of embarrassment. He then filled in a “silly questionnaire – very obvious, very slanted to the right”.

Loudermilk was then called for a video interview, which involved the identification of left-leaning celebrities, as well as figures including Borat, Cohen’s bumbling Kazakh reporter, who Loudermilk duly named.

He was also asked if he was a Trump supporter. “The funny thing is: not really. He’s just a leader. But I was like: I’m going to be an improv actor so I said: ‘Yeah, I support Trump!’ Next thing I know they’re like, ‘We want you to come out to be a part of this.’

The company flew him to Ontario, California, with an initial offer of $1,000, plus food and expenses, on top of which Loudermilk received a further $700. “It was all real secretive. They frisked me down and I got in a truck to go to a karate studio.”

Once he arrived, Loudermilk was greeted by security and informed an Israeli commander wanted to meet him. “That was a red flag. Why would Israeli commander be in America? I go in there and immediately I see this really tall guy with this horrible plastic surgery. My first thought was: that looks like Sacha Cohen. But I was like: nah, that can’t be him.”

Loudermilk’s suspicions were further raised, he says, when he met his fellow recruits. “They were old, old, old white guys who were really out of shape. Everyone’s wearing these Trump shirts. They didn’t seem like warriors. I thought: they could probably get better people for this.”

Morad then produced the top-secret new gun in the shape of large, strap-on bottom, out of which bullets are fired from the anus. Initially, Loudermilk was baffled by its logistics. “I kept thinking: no-one’s gonna wear that thing because your butt’s gonna be abnormally large. No one has a butt like that. Are you gonna put a butt on top of a butt? You can’t run in that. And you’d have to wear some really big khaki pants.”

He volunteered to be first to try the weapon out, whereupon Morad demonstrated the trigger system, which involves the pumping of an attached rubber penis. “And he kept calling it ‘shmeckel’. I’m like: that’s Sacha Cohen. At that moment I knew it.

“And I was like: oh no, I can’t break character. I’ve got to go along with it. So what I decided [was] that whatever he did, I was going to take it another step further. He’s not going to punk me; I’m going to trick him. So I decided to get into character and just have a good time because they’re paying me.”

As the plot became more “ridiculous”, says Loudermilk, so his enjoyment increased. During a scene in which he, Morad and Aboud help deliver the (prosthetic) baby, the three men become covered with fake excrement. “This big blast of pooping pap,” recalls Loudermilk – but he was reassured after inadvertently sampling some of the fluids. “I thought: ‘This doesn’t taste bad.’ So I just kind of hung in there. It was disgusting, but I was having fun.”

At the climax at the Mexican border, says Loudermilk, he handed the remote control to the drone to Aboud, “because that was boring. It was ridiculous – they’re not flying a real baby”. Instead, he “wanted to have fun with Sacha,” so he grabbed the “butt-gun”, yelling about the need to defend themselves, and eventually discharged fire, aided by Morad, who helps pump the penis trigger.

Loudermilk declines to defend Aboud’s decision to then endanger the life of the baby by dropping it from the drone, but expresses some sympathy for his unwitting co-star.

“I felt sorry for the poor fella. Because I’m all-in and he’s just kind of following me around like in a daze, like: what’s happening? He was scared to death, and obviously very uncomfortable, bless his heart.

“I’d never met the guy before, but [at times] it felt he was totally freaking out on me. So I had to make him believe that I believed all this. So I would tell him, ‘You’re being a wimp, man. Our freedom is on the line.’ I kept trying to keep him from quitting.

“But I’m surprised he didn’t figure it out. Like, come on, Michael – why would they recruit you to defend our border? Think about it.”

Loudermilk concedes his decision to maintain the pretence of being far-right once he had worked out the true nature of the project was in part because he “was a little offended. Like: really? You’re picking on these Americans that aren’t the brightest. You’re gonna make fun of them and they’re not gonna know any better. So I decided I needed to be their ally, even though I’m not that way.”

At the end of filming, Loudermilk signed a non-disclosure agreement at the request of the production company, which he says he was not given a copy of, and which mostly related to the top-secret weapons. “They came up to me after I was done and said, ‘Oh, you did so well!’ I said, ‘You get what you need? Can I talk about it?’ They said, ‘Oh no, not yet. Especially the butt-gun.’”

Loudermilk considers any such terms breached now the film is out and mentions him by name: “It’s fair game.” He adds that, after shooting, he had some qualms about not coming clean to them that he had twigged. “I felt a little bad. And that’s why I thought: I should make a little post.”

In December last year, Loudermilk posted three times on his public Facebook page about “eight hours of intense filming one on one with Sasha [sic] Cohen”, as well as twice on his Instagram, which is also public.

“But they never saw it. And the fact they missed that makes it even better to me. So I guess it’s on them.”

Last week, representatives for the film confirmed again to the Guardian that Loudermilk was not in on the joke. They have not yet responded to a further followup question on this.

The film was released nationwide in the UK on Friday, as well as in a limited run in the US. Loudermilk, who has not yet watched it, says he is unconcerned about the prospect of recriminations from anyone upset by his apparent actions.

“That people would be disgusted is a compliment. Oh, I really did a good job, huh! I pulled it off and I was so proud of myself.”

Chery 4 im Test: Laut und ungehobelt, dafür groß und billig [premium]

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Der kleinste SUV von Chery kommt zwar mit teils nervtötenden Mängeln, für den Preis ist er aber kein schlechter Deal.

Der europäische Automarkt wird zurzeit von chinesischen Hybriden überflutet. Der Chery Tiggo 4 ist ein klassisches Beispiel für die China-Welle. Auf den ersten Blick ist der SUV ein zwar generischer, aber auch recht adretter Wagen. Auf den zweiten Blick wundert man sich allerdings, wie so ein Auto VW, Stellantis und Co. zum Zittern bringt. Die Liste an kleinen und großen Konstruktionsfehlern und nervigen Details ist nämlich lang.

Gleich beim Einsteigen wird man mit einem schmerzhaft lauten Werbejingle erschreckt, der einem jedes Mal in den Ohren wehtut. Wie bei vielen chinesischen Autos hat man als großer Erwachsener auch im Tiggo 4 nicht genug Platz. Das Lenkrad kommt nicht weit genug heraus, die Knie werden beim Fahren an harte Kanten gepresst und die Tür ist so kurz, dass man die Arme nicht auflegen kann, weil die B-Säule so weit vorne ist.

Die Windgeräusche sind zudem derart stark, dass man auf der Autobahn kaum noch Musik hören kann. Laut ist auch der Motor, der über ein seltsames Getriebe nicht direkt ans Gaspedal gekoppelt ist, sondern immer wieder wild aufheult, als wäre man ein Fahranfänger, der aus Versehen ständig voll durchdrückt, ohne einen Gang einzulegen.

Und die Liste geht weiter. Der adaptive Tempomat ruckelt und eiert ständig in der Spur herum, der Blinker piepst derart nervtötend, dass man zum BMW-Fahrer mutieren könnte und nur in den wichtigsten Fällen den Richtungswechsel signalisiert. Das Getriebe ist auch beim Parken ein Albtraum; besonders im Rückwärtsgang ist es kaum möglich, harmonisch loszufahren. Die Federung ist hart, die Lenkung schwammig. Und wenn man aus dem Auto steigt, muss man die Klinke immer zweimal hintereinander ziehen, bevor sich die Tür öffnet. Wer würde sich ernsthaft für ein derart unausgereiftes Auto entscheiden?

Überraschend viele Menschen, denen Feinheiten egal und ein billiger Neuwagen wichtig ist. Für 23.490 Euro bekommt man hier nämlich neben den oben genannten Malaisen ein Auto, das Laien für doppelt so teuer halten, das genug Platz für Erwachsene auf der Rückbank und einen schicken Innenraum bietet.

Nicht zu unterschätzen ist der Stadtverbrauch von unter fünf Litern, in urbanen Gegenden fühlt sich das Auto am wohlsten. Dort fährt er öfter ganz elektrisch und wenn auch der Motor zugeschaltet wird, rennt er mit quietschenden Reifen ordentlich los, wenn man es eilig hat. Wem die europäische Wirtschaft und ein heulender Motor egal sind, der könnte mit dem Tiggo 4 glücklich werden.

Österreichs jüngste Oscar-Hoffnung, Vampire in Wien oder doch Abenteuer mit Brad Pitt? Die Kino-Highlights der Wintersaison auf einen Blick.

Valeska Grisebach ist der größte Geheimtipp des deutschen Kinos. Selten biegt sie mit einem neuen Film um die Ecke, aber jedes ihrer Werke hat Wucht. So auch das Krimi-Drama „Das geträumte Abenteuer“, das heuer in Cannes Premiere hatte. Ab Frühjahr 2027.

Österreichs jüngste Oscar-Hoffnung heißt Sandra Wollner. Die Leobnerin begeisterte die Kritik schon mit „The Trouble with Being Born“. Das Trauerstück „Everytime“ wurde nun ins Academy-Rennen für 2027 geschickt – allerdings für Deutschland. Ab 27. 11.

US-Regisseur Robert Eggers hat sich auf modernen Gothic-Horror spezialisiert – und wildert für seine Projekte im europäischen Mythenschatz. Nach „Nosferatu“ nimmt er sich jetzt den „Werwulf“ vor, Aaron Taylor-Johnson spielt die Hauptrolle. Ab 7. 1.

Zwei CIA-Killer (John Malkovich und Sam Rockwell) machen kurz vor dem Militärputsch in Chile Urlaub auf der Osterinsel – und hadern mit ihrem Beruf. Martin McDonaghs schwarze Komödie „Wild Horse Nine“, mit Parker Posey (im Bild), war heuer der Hit beim Filmfest Venedig. Ab 12. 11.

Wien, Wien, nur du allein schenkst uns das Blut unsrer Träume ein: zumindest in Ulrike Ottingers herrlich verschrobenem Film „Die Blutgräfin“, mit Isabelle Huppert in der ­Rolle einer auf modernen Pfaden wandelnden Elisabeth Báthory. Mit Birgit Minichmayr u. a. Ab 29. 10.

In Quentin Tarantinos „Once Upon a Time in … Hollywood“ (2019) war Stuntman Cliff Booth (Brad Pitt) nur der Sidekick, nun kriegt er seinen eigenen Film: „The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth“, von David Fincher nach einem Tarantino-Drehbuch inszeniert. Ab 25. 11.

He is a veteran actor of over five decades with over 180 credits to his name.

The role that he is best known for made him an icon and one of the most recognizable figures from 1980s television.

He later went on to star as the father to one of the most famous aliens of all-time,

Now he has been seen on a rare outing at 66-years-old in West Hollywood. Can you guess who he is?

„Er ist in mein Messer gelaufen“: Bei „Chicago“ in der Volksoper hopsen Kinder über Leichen [premium]

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Was tut man nicht alles, um als Mörderin an gute Schlagzeilen zu geraten. Die grausame Tat? Alles nur ein Witz im Musical „Chicago“. Unterhaltsam bedrückend. An der Wiener Volksoper mit ganz viel Körpereinsatz.

„Da habe ich einen Warnschuss abgegeben – in seinen Kopf.“ Im bekannten „Zellblocktango“ vermischen sich Humor, Satire und blutiger Ernst, wenn die Frauen im Gefängnis darüber singen, auf welche Art und Weise sie ihre Opfer ermordet haben. „He had it comin‘.“ Es war seine Schuld. Brutal geht es zu im Musical „Chicago“, in der Volksoper packend dicht von Direktorin Lotte de Beer mit der nötigen Doppelbödigkeit inszeniert. Man staunt, was die Darsteller physisch leisten in dieser Show – da wird intensiv getanzt, geturnt, gekämpft und die Treppen hinuntergefallen. Das Musical von John Kander (Musik), Fred Ebb (Gesangstexte und Buch) und Bob Fosse (Buch und legendäre Choreografie damals) feierte am Samstag in der Wiener Volksoper Premiere. Kann dieses in Revue-Nummern aufgefädelte Stück 50 Jahre nach der Uraufführung noch seine Wirkung entfalten?

„Chicago“ kokettiert mit der Vaudeville-Form, dieser Aneinanderreihung von Liedern und Szenen. Aber lose oder unzusammenhängend wie in einer echten Revue dieser Art ist hier nichts. „Chicago“ ist Gesellschaftssatire. Eröffnet wird das Musical mit einem der bekanntesten Songs: „All That Jazz.“ Klubsängerin Velma Kelly (mit unwiderstehlicher Energie: Myrthes Monteiro) entführt uns in die 1920er-Jahre. Sie selbst hat Schwester und untreuen Lover umgebracht und landet deshalb im Gefängnis. Der Traum der großen Bühne blieb der Hausfrau Roxie Hart hingegen verwehrt. Impulskontrolle steht aber auch bei ihr nicht hoch im Kurs. Sie erschießt ihren Lover, weil dieser mit ihr Schluss machen wollte, und wird ebenfalls verhaftet. Bettina Mönch beeindruckt als Roxie mit ihrer Wandlung von tapsig zu kaltblütig zu gebrochen.

Es entspinnt sich ein Wettkampf zwischen Velma und Roxie, wer die Medien und damit die Geschworenen ihrer anstehenden Mordprozesse mehr in ihren Bann ziehen kann – und es ist auch ein Wettkampf um Gunst und Aufmerksamkeit des geldgierigen Anwalts Billy Flynn (eleganter Showman, wenig furchteinflößend: Lionel von Lawrence), der die Storys in den Medien zugunsten seiner Mandantinnen am Köcheln hält. Dass beiden Damen der Galgen droht? Eine Angst, die immer nur kurz aufblitzt.

Es ist der doppelte Boden der Handlung, den Regisseurin de Beer in der Volksoper noch einmal hervorhebt. Während der Ouvertüre wird gezeigt, wie ein Faustschlag in den Bauch nur gestellt ist, wie nette Darsteller sich in ihre düsteren Rollen verwandeln. Physische Requisiten gibt es kaum: Briefe, Geld, alles wird aus Luft überreicht – vor allem gibt es keine Waffen. Geschossen wird bewusst lächerlich mit Mittel- und Zeigefinger. Gestorben wird hingegen spektakulär.

Großartige Idee in der Volksoper: Ein herziges Kindertrio sagt die einzelnen Songs an. Dadurch wird der Abgrund der Absurdität noch gesteigert, der sich da zwischen der Welt der Gangster, der mordlüsternen Vaudeville-Sängerinnen und der Nachrichtengeilheit von Reportern und Öffentlichkeit auftut. Da hopsen die drei schon einmal fröhlich über ein paar Leichen, die wenig später wieder aufstehen – alles nur Theater. Diese heiter-brutale Welt aus Gewalt und Sensationslust bröckelt aber zusehends. Und irgendwann stehen die Leichen nicht mehr auf. Plötzlich ist da dann doch Blut zu sehen. Und Roxie nimmt die Perücke ab. Ein eindrückliches Ende.

Zwischendurch geht es heiter zu. Wer lacht nicht gerne über brutale Morde? Der erste Akt zieht sich nur gegen Ende ein wenig in die Länge. In der Farbwelt setzt man an der Volksoper einen bewussten Kontrapunkt zur Revue-Erwartungshaltung. Nichts mit Rot. Alles in Grau, Schwarz, Dunkelblau und kühl mit leichten Herbst-Einsprengseln. Die rot-orange Perücke von Roxie sticht hervor. Die Kostüme (Jorine van Beek) nehmen Anleihen an der damaligen Zeit, aber sie wirken schlichter, zeitloser und bewusst oft unvollständig, um den Showcharakter zu unterstreichen.

Die Drehbühne mit Wandaufbau bietet einen kargen, aber wirkungsvollen Raum für die Szenen. Eine große Treppe dient als Kulisse für Show, Knast und Gericht. Die Rückseite bietet Platz für intimere Monologe und Kletterei auf der Gerüstwand. Chor, Ballett und Musicalensemble formen sich dabei zu einem Ganzen in „Chicago“, die Grenzen dieser drei Einheiten zerfließen. Das Orchester (musikalische Leitung: Maayan Franco) swingt und tönt präzise aus dem Graben und verschmilzt in der berühmten Gerichtsszene beeindruckend mit dem Ensemble.

Gesungen wird übrigens auf Englisch mit deutschen Übertiteln. „All der Jazz“ hätte natürlich weniger Sex-Appeal als „All that Jazz“. Durch die englischen Songtexte tritt der Nummernrevue-Aspekt noch deutlicher zum Vorschein und schafft Distanz. In manchen Songs würde deutscher Text aber mehr Nähe zum Publikum schaffen, zum Beispiel wenn Roxies Ehemann in „Mr. Cellophane“ darüber singt, wie wenig er von der Welt beachtet wird.

Jakob Semotan hat als treuherziger und naiver Amos Hart das Mitleid des Publikums auf seiner Seite. Sein letzter Abgang im zweiten Akt ist einer der lustigsten und traurigsten Momente im ganzen Stück. Ein weiterer Volksopern-Publikumsliebling ist Katia Ledoux, sie brilliert in ihren Auftritten als Mama Morton, hat Knast, Outfit, Publikum und Stimme fest im Griff. Ledoux’ stimmliche Wandelbarkeit durch die Musiktheater-Genres ist immer wieder beeindruckend.

Was bleibt in Erinnerung von dem Abend an der Volksoper? Großartige Hauptdarstellerinnen, eine bis in die letzte Reihe spürbare Körperlichkeit aller Darstellenden, hier schont sich niemand! Die berühmten Melodien natürlich. Und eine optisch eindrückliche Inszenierung, die zwar ganz auf Farbe verzichtet, aber „Chicago“ in allen Facetten ausleuchtet.

Musik: John KanderGesangstexte: Fred EbbBuch: Fred Ebb & Bob Fosse

My video of the moment Thomas Markle received letter from Meghan accusing him of abusing young girls. I was there and know exactly what happened next. And I can reveal secret of why he's only speaking out now, by CAROLINE GRAHAM

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

By CAROLINE GRAHAM, US EDITOR THE MAIL ON SUNDAY

Published: 11:13 GMT, 25 October 2026 | Updated: 19:05 GMT, 25 October 2026

Thomas Markle had only just managed to sit upright in his hospital bed for the first time since having his leg amputated when there was a knock at the door.

Two women walked in. One was clutching a white envelope bearing Mr Markle's name.

It was December last year and this was the moment he discovered that his estranged daughter, Meghan, had sent him a letter.

What neither of us could have imagined was that the envelope would become the focus of a fresh and deeply troubling chapter in the extraordinary saga of the fractured Markle family.

It has now emerged that, according to The Sun, Meghan's letter contained the sensational allegation that she 'knew her father had abused young girls' and that she wrote he would never meet his grandchildren, Archie, seven, and Lilibet, five.

I had absolutely no inkling that the innocuous-looking white envelope contained such a bombshell.

Out of respect for Meghan's privacy, I never asked Mr Markle what the letter said.

But I vividly remember the bizarre circumstances in which it was delivered because it was a scene so surreal it has stayed with me ever since.

The moment in December last year that Thomas Markle discovered that his estranged daughter, Meghan, had sent him a letter

It has now emerged that, according to The Sun, Meghan's letter contained the sensational allegation that she 'knew her father had abused young girls'

I flew to the Philippines last December at Mr Markle's urging. We became friends in 2018 and he asked me to come after doctors discovered a massive blood clot that meant his left leg would have to be amputated below the knee.

By the time I arrived in Cebu – the city to which he and his son Tom Jr had moved in January 2025 in search of a 'fresh start' – Mr Markle was in intensive care.

It was in Cebu that Tom Jr allegedly discovered a stash of child sex images on his father's computer, phone and hard drives, claims Mr Markle has vehemently denied.

A few days after my arrival, Mr Markle was transferred to a private room at the University of Cebu Medical Centre.

For a then 81-year-old man who had already suffered two heart attacks and a stroke, his recovery was remarkable.

I remember a physiotherapist entering the room and announcing: 'OK, Mr Tom, it's time for you to get moving.'

He manoeuvred him from a prone position into a sitting position on the edge of the bed. We were smiling at this small but significant milestone when there was a knock at the door.

In walked two women who introduced themselves as a lawyer and the hospital's president.

There had been no warning of their arrival.

Mr Markle was sitting on the bed with the stump of his leg exposed, still wrapped in a bloodied bandage. Instinctively I reached for a sheet to cover it and preserve his dignity.

The lawyer was carrying a clutch of legal documents. The women explained that they had come to deliver a letter from his daughter Meghan and that Mr Markle would have to sign paperwork authorising them to film the encounter so that footage could be sent to Meghan's office as proof that the sealed letter had been handed to him.

I immediately offered to leave the room but Mr Markle asked me to stay.

Thomas Markle and Filipino nurse Rio Canedo, 46, at the rehab centre in Cebu, Philippines

'If you are going to video this, then I would like Caroline to video it too,' he told the lawyer.

Here was an elderly man, seriously ill and heavily medicated with powerful painkillers, being asked to sign legal documents before receiving a letter from his own daughter.

The hospital's president performatively handed him the envelope bowing slightly as she said: 'Mr Markle, I have been asked to hand you this personally by your daughter Meghan. It is sealed. I need to make sure you know it is sealed.'

There was a nod towards the iPhone camera being held by the lawyer.

Once they knew the encounter had been successfully captured on tape the two women left as quickly as they had arrived, leaving Mr Markle and I alone with the glaringly white envelope lying on his hospital bed.

He told me he was not ready to read Meghan's letter and asked me to move it out of his sight. I put it on a plastic table next to his uneaten lunch.

For the next four hours he fretted about opening it. He told me he was 'scared' it would 'contain bad news'.

Eventually he decided he could put it off no longer.

He asked me to hand him the envelope. Once again, I offered to leave the room while he read it.

He read the letter in silence, saying nothing. He shook his head several times. Then he folded the letter up, slipped it back into its envelope and asked me to put it in the wardrobe.

And that was that. He told me nothing about its contents. I asked no questions.

Tom Jr, Meghan's brother, claimed to have found the child-abuse material packed up in boxes at his father's Philippines home

The letter went into his bag in the wardrobe containing the dirty clothes he'd arrived at the hospital in and we never discussed it again. Until now.

Following The Sun's revelations about the letter on Sunday, I telephoned Mr Markle, who is now living in a rehabilitation centre in Florida after returning to the US in May to continue his medical treatment.

He told me he has 'a vague memory' of receiving the letter but cannot recall what it said.

Mr Markle has since been diagnosed with dementia, a condition he has authorised the Daily Mail to report.

He also gave permission for us to share the video of the extraordinary hospital encounter.

'I remember getting the letter but I don't remember anything else about it,' he said.  'I'm happy for people to see the video. I have nothing to hide.'

I asked him about The Sun's report that Meghan said in the letter that she knew of allegations he was a child sex abuser: 'I'm not a paedophile. I've never done anything bad to a child. Never.

'If Meghan says that's what she wrote, then she's probably right. I was very sick. I have memory issues. I know there was a letter, but I don't remember what it said.'

Mr Markle left the Philippines in May, leaving most of his possessions behind. He intended to return later this year once he had learned to walk using a prosthetic limb.

He has strenuously denied his son's allegations about the child sex abuse images, describing the claims as 'terrible lies'.

He maintains the computers said to contain the material were out of his control for nearly two years following their move to Cebu.

'The stuff was boxed up in Mexico and put on a container ship. I kept asking Tom for my computers, but he said they were in storage.'

Meanwhile, Tom Jr, a man I also have known for years, insists his father is in 'denial' and claims he acted because the material he found was so horrific he had to report it.

Authorities in the Philippines have launched an enquiry and the FBI in the US is likely to follow suit this week.

Mr Markle has said he will fully co-operate with authorities to clear his name and has even offered to take a lie detector test.

For now the letter that arrived under such weird circumstances in that Filipino hospital room remains missing its contents, beyond what has been reported by The Sun, unconfirmed by Mr Markle himself or, as of now, the Duchess of Sussex.

Mr Markle claims he remembers nothing of the letter's contents but I will never forget the dramatic, and frankly weird, way it was delivered.

Lara Croft is 30! We look at the video game icon’s biggest blunders

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

We all know the highlights of the tomb raider’s storied career – but what about the lowlights? We dig deep to unearth the archaeologist’s less glorious moments

Happy birthday, Lara! This month marks 30 years since Ms Croft first swan-dived into our lives with her unique blend of athletic prowess, archaeological expertise and giant knockers. More than 100 million Tomb Raider games have been sold since. We all remember the good times: the bold design and sheer playability of the original titles; the establishment of Lara as a 90s icon, embodying both lads’ mag culture and girl power; the way she made games cool, even appearing on the cover of the Face. We’ve had blockbuster movies, big budget reboots, and shiny reissues. And who could forget the time we got to powerwash her house?

But no one gets to 30 without a few skeletons in the closet – or in Lara’s case, butlers in the freezer. So here’s a look at the darker parts of her past.

Lara’s image has been used to hawk a multitude of products over the years, from cereal and soft drinks to reasonably priced Spanish hatchbacks. Low points include the 1999 promo for a German women’s magazine in which Lara tells us what she really, really wants isn’t the Dagger of Xian or the MacGuffin of Atlantis, but a big meringuey wedding dress.

See also the weird Dreamcast advert where Lara talks about the console as if it’s a jar of anti-ageing moisturiser. This led an entire generation of women to try reducing the appearance of fine lines by rubbing Sega hardware on their faces. Because she’s worth it? Let’s hope the money was.

Back in 1996, no one predicted how successful this quiet game about a posh archaeologist made by a small team in Derby would be. But once publisher Eidos realised it was in possession of a goose that laid golden eggs, it launched an intensive battery farming operation. Core Design was forced to churn out a new Tomb Raider title every year.

Four games in, the developers had had enough. In 1999 they decided to bury Lara under a collapsing pyramid at the end of The Last Revelation. News of her demise was tucked away towards the end of the design document, the Core team having taken a punt that their publisher overlords wouldn’t bother reading that far.

They were correct. The Eidos execs were furious when they learned of Lara’s fate, but it was too late to change the game’s ending. Instead they put pressure on Core to find a narrative workaround so they could squeeze out one last PS1 adventure. The result was Tomb Raider: Chronicles, which begins with a memorial service for Lara and continues in a series of flashbacks. It is rubbish.

Lara’s first PS2 outing, The Angel of Darkness, was released in 2003. It was aiming for mature and edgy, but landed slap bang in the middle of dull and depressing.

The game missed the point of what players loved about Tomb Raider – the thrill of exploring exotic locations and abandoned temples. No one asked to wander around a gloomy version of Paris that could pass for Croydon, talking to sex workers and pawn shop owners while dressed in double denim.

It didn’t help that the game was full of bugs and had awful controls. A terrible new strength mechanic forced the player to build up Lara’s muscle before she could perform previously simple tasks, like moving a crate.

To top it all off, a secondary protagonist was introduced: tiresome edgelord Kurtis Trent, whose toxic masculinity ruined the party for everyone. Which brings us to…

Another key appeal of the original Tomb Raiders was the feeling of being a solo adventurer exploring a strange world, never knowing when the eerie silence would be shattered by the snarl of a hungry wolf. No one ever wished Lara was accompanied by a bloke in a padded gilet saying: “Hey, I wonder if that rusty key we found will fit in this rusty lock?”

But when Crystal Dynamics took over from Core in the 00s, they decided Lara needed some annoying sidekicks. The worst offender is Jonah, who makes his debut in the 2013 reboot. Not only is he feckless and mansplainy, he actively creates work for Lara by being an absolute liability. Over the course of their adventures he gets trapped, tortured, stabbed, kidnapped, knocked unconscious, shipwrecked, buried in an avalanche, caught in a tsunami, falsely declared dead, and almost killed in a plane crash. Ironically, the only calamity that doesn’t befall Jonah is being swallowed by a whale. A shame.

None of the Tomb Raider films are stellar – although many middle-aged women have a soft spot for the first Angelina Jolie outing, in which our hero dispatches an army of goons without having to leave the house, or even get out of her pyjamas.

The follow-up, The Cradle of Life, is absolute twaddle. Philip French called it “repetitive and curiously inert”. The BBC’s Nev Pierce went further: “Incoherent, dumb and stupefyingly bland … It’s as though they wanted to make a terrible film.”

Pierce went on to describe Lara as a “pumped-up Judith Chalmers”, which points to a missed casting opportunity – who wouldn’t want to watch the affable host of Wish You Were Here…? scale Angkor Wat with a grappling hook?

Although Chalmers never got the chance, plenty of other women were cast in the role of real-life Lara over the years. They included model Nell McAndrew, who donned the turquoise tank top to promote Tomb Raider III.

Things went tits up when she also appeared in Playboy magazine, the game’s logo proudly displayed on the cover alongside the words: “LARA CROFT NUDE.”

Core Design sued, unhappy that the video game character they had endowed with comically enormous breasts was being sexualised. They won – a judge ordered stickers be applied to 20,000 copies of the magazine, covering up the offending logo. It is not known how badly this affected sales among the Playboy readers eager to catch an illicit glimpse of unlicensed intellectual property.

The 2013 Tomb Raider reboot was solid and polished. However, its creators failed to understand Lara’s appeal as a strong, enigmatic main character, particularly for female gamers.

Art director Brian Horton talked about wanting to make players “care for” Lara. He said her torturous experiences and ripped clothing would show “beauty and vulnerability”, suggesting this was “sexy in its own way”.

The controversy really kicked off after a trailer was released showing a man pinning Lara against a wall and groping her. Executive producer Ron Rosenberg said the sexual assault would make players want to “protect” the game’s protagonist, and “root for her in a way you wouldn’t root for a male character”. This focus on the male gaze and demotion of Lara to damsel in distress represented a staggering failure to read the room, and to notice there were women in it.

There’s no doubt that Lara has endured some dark times over the last three decades. In fact, she’s had it harder than any other veteran video game character. No one ever suggested Solid Snake would have more appeal if he sported a gigantic pair of testicles, or insisted on giving Mario daddy issues. (Although, as annoying sidekicks go, Sonic the Hedgehog’s mate Tails gives Jonah a run for his gold rings.)

Yet here she is, still standing, with another adventure arriving next year. Perhaps this one will tell Lara’s true origin story: how she rose to prominence not because of her prominent chest, or past traumas, but because she was the cool, enigmatic star of a bloody great game.

Lara Croft remains a leading icon of gaming culture, despite the repeated failure of sexist developers and unimaginative marketing teams to understand her power. As someone born after she was created might say, that’s why Lara Croft is the GOAT – not just the greatest of all tomb raiders, but the greatest of all time.

Il se plaint de douleurs abdominales et de vomissements, les médecins découvrent 30 clés métalliques et des aimants dans son estomac

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Article rédigé par La rédaction de La Dépêche du Midi

l'essentiel Un homme de 35 ans s'est présenté aux urgences, en Somalie, pour des douleurs abdominales. Une radioscopie a révélé qu'il avait en effet pas moins de 32 objets métalliques (dont 30 clés) coincés dans l'estomac. Il a pu être opéré et transféré vers une unité de soins psychiatriques.

L'histoire paraît à peine croyable. Futura-sciences rapporte qu'en Somalie, un homme âgé de 35 ans a subi une intervention suite à la détection d'une masse métallique nichée dans son estomac. Le patient s'était présenté aux urgences de lui-même, se plaignant de douleurs abdominales et de vomissements à répétition.

À lire aussi : Il souffre le martyre après une opération, mais l'hôpital le renvoie chez lui à six reprises : les chirurgiens avaient oublié une agrafe dans son ventre

Il s'est vu prescrire une radio pour comprendre ce qui pouvait ainsi le gêner et l'image a révélé la présence d'objets métalliques dans son estomac. Une endoscopie digestive haute a ensuite permis, grâce à une exploration plus poussée, de découvrir qu'il s'agissait d'un amas de 30 clés et de deux aimants, ayant provoqué de légères lésions de la muqueuse gastrique.

À lire aussi : "Une chance incroyable" Il consulte pour des douleurs au ventre : les médecins découvrent un thermomètre à mercure coincé dans son estomac depuis 20 ans

Nos confrères de Futura-sciences expliquent que les équipes médicales sont parvenues à extraire tous les objets de l'estomac du patient à l'aide d'une pince à griffe et d'une sonde panier, le tout, sans endommager le système digestif de l'homme.

À lire aussi : Ils opèrent une femme de 82 ans pour une occlusion intestinale : les médecins découvrent un cloporte vivant dans son intestin

Ce dernier a ensuite été gardé à l'hôpital, pour un temps d'observation, avant d'être orienté vers une unité de prise en charge des patients souffrant de troubles psychiatriques. En effet, ce passage aux urgences a permis de lui diagnostiquer une schizophrénie, ce qui pourrait expliquer l'ingestion volontaire d'objets métalliques.

Des studios de cinéma d'Hollywood à Decazeville, il n'y a qu'un pas... Comment les studios de cinéma laissent-ils une empreinte dans cette commune de l'Aveyron ?

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Article rédigé par Correspondant de l'Aveyron

l'essentiel Les studios Paramount, basés à Hollywood en Californie, s’associent à l’artiste OakOak, grande figure de l’art urbain français, pour la sortie mondiale du film *Street Fighter*, réalisé par Kitao Sakurai. Cette association a permis de réaliser de nouvelles œuvres de street-art à Decazeville dans l'Aveyron.

Durant le mois de septembre dernier, l’artiste Oakoak a posé ses bombes et ses pochoirs sur les murs et le sol de Decazeville pour une série d’œuvres réalisées, en partenariat avec Paramount Pictures International - dont la maison mère, située à Hollywood (Californie), reste l’un des plus anciens studios du cinéma américain encore en activité - et Paramount France. Oakoak, qui tient à garder son anonymat, est un artiste français originaire de Saint-Étienne.

Sur les six fresques prévues dans le cadre de cette collaboration, trois ont été réalisées à Decazeville et trois à Paris. Cette répartition en dit long sur la place que le territoire occupe dans l’esprit de l’artiste, et sur la confiance que lui accorde Paramount en validant l’association avec Decazeville communauté pour créer une campagne visuelle d’envergure mondiale. Le film sort dans toutes les salles de cinéma et il est visible actuellement à la Strada, à Decazeville.

Les trois créations de Decazeville mettent en scène des personnages emblématiques de la saga, en jouant avec les supports fournis par Decazeville communauté. Sur un passage piéton, Ken apparaît en trompe-l’œil : vêtu de son kimono rouge, il semble jaillir le poing enflammé levé vers le ciel ayant préalablement envoyé balader le passage piéton. Sur le bitume, Ryu, bandeau rouge au front, lance son Hadoken : le coup a fait éclater la chaussée et laisse un cratère bordé d’éclats d’asphalte.

À lire aussi : Annoncé en toute fin de conseil municipal à Decazeville, le maire a fait une annonce concernant l'avenir de la cuisine centrale située dans la ville

Enfin, sur le mur de parpaings des anciennes halles Vallourec, Blanka bondit au milieu d’éclairs, la paroi fissurée derrière lui, à côté du célèbre logo du jeu. Ces œuvres invitent le public à entrer dans la scène.

Avec un public nombreux et une notoriété qui dépasse largement les frontières nationales, Oakoak donne à cet événement une résonance internationale. Ce partenariat entre un géant hollywoodien et un artiste d’art urbain illustre une tendance de fond : les grandes marques, la publicité et les studios misent de plus en plus sur l’authenticité et la puissance visuelle du street art pour toucher un public mondial. Ils n’hésitent plus à sortir des grandes métropoles pour la trouver là où elle s’exprime le mieux.

Un artiste stéphanois a produit 3 œuvres street art en lien direct avec Paramount pour la sortie du film "Street Fighter". Ses créations decazevilloises ont largement dépassé le million de vues sur les réseaux sociaux. Photo C.C Pour les observateurs du secteur, cette répartition est l’élément le plus significatif de la campagne. « Oui, un territoire hors métropole peut attirer un projet et une visibilité de niveau international dès lors qu’il dispose de professionnels solides, d’une identité artistique claire et d’une vraie capacité d’accompagnement artistique, y compris dans des conditions exigeantes. Cette capacité opérationnelle a permis d’accueillir sur le territoire le cœur d’un événement piloté depuis New York et Paris ».

À lire aussi : Créé pour la première fois en 1988, le quartier de Saint-Roch à Decazeville perpétue son spectacle d'année en année ; il revient pour une nouvelle édition ce week-end.

Une chose est sûre : quand les projecteurs du monde entier se tournent vers le film ce mois-ci, chaque fresque, photographiée, partagée et commentée, deviendra une vitrine pour Decazeville communauté, ses habitants, son projet street art et son dynamisme culturel.

Melindres Gallegos: Secretos y Recetas del Dulce Tradicional

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Si hay un bocado que transporte a cualquiera directamente a las romerías y ferias de Galicia, son sin duda los melindres. Estos pequeños tesoros de la repostería gallega, con su forma de rosquilla y ese toque anisado tan particular, son mucho más que un simple postre; representan la esencia de las reuniones familiares y las fiestas populares de la comunidad.

Aunque se disfrutan en diversas comarcas, tienen un arraigo especial en Ferrolterra y sobre todo en Melide, donde se han convertido en un símbolo local. Es curioso cómo una receta aparentemente sencilla, basada en ingredientes básicos, puede variar tanto según el maestro pastelero o la zona, guardando cada familia su propio secreto para lograr esa textura seca que se deshace en la boca.

La historia de este dulce está profundamente ligada al Camino de Santiago y a la zona de Melide. Se dice que fueron los monjes de los monasterios cercanos quienes perfeccionaron la receta para crear un alimento energético y resistente que los peregrinos pudieran transportar sin que se estropeara durante su camino hacia Santiago de Compostela. Con el paso de los siglos, lo que empezó como un sustento para el viajero pasó a ser una tradición doméstica transmitida de abuelos a nietos.

En la actualidad, el melindre es el protagonista absoluto del segundo domingo de mayo en Melide, donde se celebra una fiesta gastronómica dedicada a la repostería tradicional. En este evento se rinde homenaje a la maestría de los obradores locales, destacando el melindre junto a otras delicias como el rico y el almendrado, manteniendo vivo un legado artesanal que resiste el paso del tiempo.

Para preparar unos melindres auténticos, no hace falta buscar ingredientes exóticos, ya que la magia reside en la calidad de los productos básicos. La base común suele incluir harina de trigo de repostería, huevos (especialmente yemas) y mantequilla o aceite, dependiendo de la variante. El elemento indispensable que define su identidad es el licor de anís, que aporta ese aroma tan nostálgico.

En cuanto al glaseado, que es donde reside gran parte de la dificultad, se utiliza una mezcla de azúcar y agua para crear un almíbar. Algunas versiones tradicionales añaden una clara de huevo montada al almíbar para conseguir un acabado blanco y opaco, mientras que otras buscan una capa más traslúcida y cristalina que deje ver el dorado del dulce.

Esta versión es ideal para quienes buscan un resultado más ligero y sencillo de ejecutar. Para empezar, se deben batir las yemas con la mantequilla derretida y el anís, incorporando la harina tamizada poco a poco hasta obtener una masa homogénea. Es fundamental amasar durante unos diez minutos hasta que la pasta esté lisa y no se pegue a las manos, dejándola reposar bajo un film durante una hora para que la masa se asiente.

Para darles forma, se crean pequeños cilindros que luego se enrollan sobre sí mismos formando un nudo o un aro. El horneado se realiza a una temperatura alta, generalmente entre 200ºC y 220ºC, durante unos 10 minutos, hasta que estén ligeramente dorados. Una vez fríos, se bañan en un almíbar de agua y azúcar calentado a fuego medio. El truco aquí es ser muy rápido con el glaseado, ya que el azúcar solidifica en cuestión de segundos al entrar en contacto con el dulce.

Para los amantes de los sabores más intensos, existe la versión frita, muy común en las ferias. En este caso, la masa puede llevar levadura química (Royal) y aceite de girasol u oliva en lugar de mantequilla. La preparación implica mezclar los huevos, el azúcar y el anís, añadiendo la harina y la levadura hasta formar una masa consistente. En lugar de nudos complejos, a veces se forman bolitas con un hueco en el centro hecho con el dedo.

La fritura debe hacerse en abundante aceite caliente pero sin llegar a humear para evitar que se quemen por fuera y queden crudos por dentro. Tras escurrirlos en papel absorbente, se procede al glaseado. Una técnica muy efectiva es montar una clara a punto de nieve e incorporarla al almíbar hirviendo, batiendo enérgicamente para crear una glasa blanca y espesa donde se sumergen los melindres rápidamente antes de dejarlos secar en una rejilla.

Si utilizas una Thermomix, puedes optimizar la mezcla de aceites y aromas, pero recuerda que para cantidades grandes es mejor terminar el amasado a mano para controlar la consistencia. Un detalle clave es tamizar siempre la harina para evitar grumos y asegurar que todos los ingredientes estén a temperatura ambiente antes de comenzar la elaboración.

Para que el glaseado quede perfecto, evita mojar los dulces uno a uno si tienes mucha cantidad; es preferible verter el almíbar sobre el conjunto de melindres en un bol y remover con rapidez. Para conservarlos, lo ideal es utilizar un recipiente hermético, ya que al ser una pasta seca, se mantienen en buen estado durante varios días sin necesidad de refrigeración, lo que los hace perfectos para llevar de excursión.

Estos dulces gallegos representan la unión perfecta entre la historia medieval de los peregrinos y la repostería casera actual, ofreciendo dos caminos de elaboración, ya sea mediante la ligereza del horno o la intensidad de la fritura. El secreto final reside siempre en el equilibrio entre la masa neutra y la dulzura del glaseado anisado, logrando un bocado tradicional que sigue cautivando paladares en cada romería y sobremesa de Galicia.

FOX fairy tale: Sean Hannity, Ainsley Earhardt wed...

📅 Oct 25, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Published: 04:30 GMT, 25 October 2026 | Updated: 14:12 GMT, 25 October 2026

Fox hosts Sean Hannity and Ainsley Earhardt tied the knot Saturday before a star-studded party at one of the nation’s most exclusive private clubs, the Daily Mail can reveal.

The cable news favorites said ‘I do’ at the storied Church of Bethesda-by-the-Sea in Palm Beach, Florida, after a near-two-year engagement.

Earhardt, 50, walked down the aisle in a stunning Oscar de la Renta gown surrounded by family, friends and some of the biggest names in politics and entertainment.

The nuptials continued Saturday evening with a lavish reception at the nearby Everglades Club, an elite members-only haunt that guards its exclusivity so fiercely that patrons are barred from bringing cell phones onto the premises.

Guests included President Donald Trump’s chief of staff Susie Wiles, Eric and Lara Trump, casino mogul Steve Wynn, Florida Governor Ron DeSantis and country music icon Lee Greenwood, all toasting the smitten couple with champagne and cocktails.

A huge Fox News contingent included Earhardt’s Fox & Friends co-hosts Steve Doocy, Lawrence Jones and Brian Kilmeade, plus Laura Ingraham, Bret Baier, newly-minted Fox News contributor Karoline Leavitt, CEO Suzanne Scott and network president and executive editor Jay Wallace.

The wedding weekend had kicked off Friday night with a VIP party at Donald Trump's Mar-a-Lago estate attended by the President and First Lady Melania Trump, Secretary of State Marco Rubio and Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr.

‘My heart is overflowing with gratitude and joy,’ Earhardt said in a statement released via Fox News.‘To celebrate this beautiful season of our lives surrounded by the people we love most is truly a dream come true.‘

Fox hosts Sean Hannity and Ainsley Earhardt tied the knot Saturday before a star-studded party at one of the nation’s most exclusive private clubs (source: Hannity Family)

Ainsley Earhardt and Sean Hannity (source: Twitter/X)

Sean Hannity and Ainsley Earhardt arrive at The Mar-a-Lago Club, Sunday, Feb. 1, 2026

Earhardt pictured with president Donald Trump

The Everglades Club, a playground for the town’s wealthy and well-connected elite, was chosen for the reception for its grandeur and privacy.

The historic venue is tucked away behind Worth Avenue, a ritzy shopping enclave lined with designer boutiques, art galleries and grand mansions.

It dates to the final months of the Great War, when its founder, Paris Singer, heir to the Singer sewing-machine empire, set out to build a convalescent center for wounded American servicemen.

He enlisted architect Addison Mizner, who drew on Spanish and Italian architecture to produce an ornate complex of stucco walls, courtyards and intricate ironwork, all hallmarks of the Mediterranean Revival style that would become synonymous with Palm Beach.

Construction began in 1918, but the Armistice ended the war before the project could fulfil its original purpose and Singer changed course, opening the property as an invitation-only members’ club in February 1919.

The original compound included eight elegant villas, tennis courts and a yacht basin, while a golf course was added in 1920.

Lawrence Jones, Ainsley Earhardt and Brian Kilmeade with Kid Rock as he visits "Fox & Friends" at Fox News Channel Studios on October 16, 2026

Hannity split from his first wife Jill Rhodes, 64, with whom he shares children, Patrick, 27, and Merri, 25, in 2015

Within two years, membership was capped at 500 well-heeled patrons.

Businessman Jack C. Massey, who built a fortune through companies including Kentucky Fried Chicken and Hospital Corporation of America, was a member, as was Eliza Osgood Vanderbilt Webb, granddaughter of railroad magnate Cornelius Vanderbilt.

By 1999, the initiation fee alone was rumored to be $35,000, according to the Palm Beach Daily News.

The club still doesn’t have a website.Anyone hoping to stage a wedding celebration at the Everglades has to either be a member themselves or have an existing member sponsor them, a worker explained when the Daily Mail rang the club last week.

But that evidently wasn’t a problem for the well-connected Hannity, who moved to South Florida full time in 2024 and owns two townhouses in Palm Beach and a waterfront mansion in nearby Manalapan that he bought for a combined $44 million.

Earhardt stayed put in New York but the couple made their long-distance engagement work by crisscrossing the country on romantic weekend trips.

'They had only seen each other on weekends given Ainsley lives in the city and Sean was on Long Island,’ a friend previously told the Daily Mail.

'The only difference now is that they will be traveling by plane instead of a car back and forth to Long Island.'

'We are overjoyed and so thankful to our families for all of their love and support during this wonderful time in our lives.’

Hannity joined Fox when it launched in 1996 and his eponymous nightly show has made him one of the most influential figures in conservative media and the longest-running primetime cable news host in history.
